People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]

Yeah… I guess I have. I’ve skipped a few dance nights and mentoring sessions because it just felt like too much to get myself there. And at work, I’m… kind of falling behind on tickets because I keep rechecking simple things, so other people have had to help more than they should.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’ve just been feeling really wound up most of the time. Like my body is sort of bracing for something bad, even when I’m just at my desk… or trying to relax. It’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. It’s not really images so much as thoughts. Like I’ll know I already checked a spreadsheet or sent an email, but it keeps looping anyway. Then one “what if” turns into a bunch more before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to do certain things over and over, like checking or cleaning?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I might double-check something if I’m worried, but I don’t feel like I have to do it over and over in a ritual kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any sudden, intense episodes of fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I get really uncomfortable and keyed up, but I wouldn’t say it suddenly peaks like that within minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or places because they make you feel anxious or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I’ve skipped a few dance nights and mentoring sessions because it just felt like too much to get myself there. And at work, I’m… kind of falling behind on tickets because I keep rechecking simple things, so other people have had to help more than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. It’s usually worries, not like random thoughts. I’ll tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, but it keeps coming back. Then one “what if” turns into a few more, and it takes me a while to pull myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they do come in when I don’t want them to. Like I’ll tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, but it just keeps looping. Then one “what if” turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to perform actions in a specific, orderly way?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I like things to be right, but I wouldn’t say I have to do them in some exact order.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive and focused on a specific concern, like fear of contamination or harm to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I don’t think it’s about contamination or hurting anyone. It can start with the condo stuff, but then it jumps around. It’s like it doesn’t stay on one thing. I’ll worry about the condo, and then it’s whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up a support call at work, or how I’m supposed to save money and actually move forward. It just kind of keeps shifting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge, like you can't relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I mean, I like things to be right, but I wouldn’t say I have to do them in some exact order.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive and focused on a specific concern, like fear of contamination or harm to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I don’t think it’s about contamination or hurting anyone. It’s like it doesn’t stay on one thing. I’ll worry about the condo, and then it’s whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up a support call at work, or how I’m supposed to save money and actually move forward. It just kind of keeps shifting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge, like you can't relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I feel kind of wound up most of the time, like my body’s bracing for something, even if I’m just sitting at my desk or trying to relax. Then one “what if” turns into a bunch more, and it takes me a while to pull out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually worry about a wide range of things, or is it mostly focused on one or two specific areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s more a range of things, I guess. It used to feel more about the condo, but now it jumps around… like whether my cousin’s upset with me, or if I’ll mess up a support call, or how I’m supposed to save money and move forward.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area in your life where you feel most of your worries are centered, or do you feel like your worries are spread out across many different areas?\n\nPatient: It’s spread out now. It used to be more about the condo, but lately it jumps around… like whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’ll mess up a support call, or how I’m supposed to save money and actually move forward.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries could be grouped into one main area of your life, or are they really spread across many different parts?\n\nPatient: It’s… pretty spread out. It’s not really images so much as thoughts. Like I’ll know I already checked a spreadsheet or sent an email, but it keeps looping anyway. Then one “what if” turns into a bunch more before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to do certain things over and over, like checking or cleaning?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I might double-check something if I’m worried, but I don’t feel like I have to do it over and over in a ritual kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any sudden, intense episodes of fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I get really uncomfortable and keyed up, but I wouldn’t say it suddenly peaks like that within minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or places because they make you feel anxious or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I’ve skipped a few dance nights and mentoring sessions because it just felt like too much to get myself there. And at work, I’m… kind of falling behind on tickets because I keep rechecking simple things, so other people have had to help more than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. It’s usually worries, not like random thoughts. I’ll tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, but it keeps coming back. Then one “what if” turns into a few more, and it takes me a while to pull myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, meaning they come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… they do come in when I don’t want them to. Like I’ll tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, but it just keeps looping. Then one “what if” turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to perform actions in a specific, orderly way?\n\nPatient: Not really… I don’t think so. I mean, I like things to be right, but I wouldn’t say I have to do them in some exact order.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive and focused on a specific concern, like fear of contamination or harm to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I don’t think it’s about contamination or hurting anyone. It’s more just… worries, I guess, but not that specific.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific things or situations that usually trigger your worries or make you feel more wound up?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s not just one thing. It can start with the condo stuff, but then it jumps around. Like, whether my cousin’s upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up on a support call at work, or… money, and whether I’m actually moving forward at all. Stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about everyday things, like work, finances, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. It’s like it doesn’t stay on one thing. I’ll worry about the condo, and then it’s whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’m going to mess up a support call at work, or how I’m supposed to save money and actually move forward. It just kind of keeps shifting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge, like you can't relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot. I feel kind of wound up most of the time, like my body’s bracing for something, even if I’m just sitting at my desk or trying to relax. It’s hard to really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are repetitive and unwanted, and that you try to suppress or ignore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sort of. It’s more like the same worries keep looping. Even if I tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet or sent the email, my mind keeps going back to it. Then one “what if” turns into a bunch more, and it takes me a while to pull out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually worry about a wide range of things, or is it mostly focused on one or two specific areas of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s more a range of things, I guess. It used to feel more about the condo, but now it jumps around… like whether my cousin’s upset with me, or if I’ll mess up a support call, or how I’m supposed to save money and move forward.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area in your life where you feel most of your worries are centered, or do you feel like your worries are spread out across many different areas?\n\nPatient: It’s spread out now. It used to be more about the condo, but lately it jumps around… like whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I’ll mess up a support call, or how I’m supposed to save money and actually move forward.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries could be grouped into one main area of your life, or are they really spread across many different parts?\n\nPatient: It’s… pretty spread out. Like it doesn’t just stay on the condo anymore. It’ll jump from worrying if my cousin’s upset with me, to whether I’ll mess up a support call, to money and whether I’m actually moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are irrational or unreasonable, but you can't stop thinking about them anyway?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know some of it doesn’t really make sense. Like I’ll tell myself I already checked the spreadsheet, or I already sent the email, but it still keeps looping. Then one “what if” turns into a bunch more, and it’s hard to stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are worse than they need to be, considering the actual situations you're worrying about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It feels bigger than the situation most of the time. Like it doesn’t just stay on one thing—it jumps from the condo to my cousin, or work calls, or money… and then it feels like I’m worrying way past what’s actually happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
